# Worklog

This registry connects repository history to the roadmap and to optional detail records that preserve reasoning too large for a commit subject. Rows are chronological, and commit subjects are copied verbatim from Git.

## Ongoing policy

- Add every substantive commit to this registry with its short SHA, commit date, exact subject, related roadmap issue when known, and detail-file link when one exists.
- Add a detail file from [TEMPLATE.md](TEMPLATE.md) when a commit carries durable reasoning: approach, notable challenges, decisions or rejected alternatives, or follow-up work. Historical detail files are not backfilled; they begin going forward.
- A substantive commit must be indexed by an immediately following ledger update. A commit that changes only `docs/worklog/**` and the reciprocal commit cell in `docs/roadmap/README.md` must use the exact subject prefix `docs(worklog):` and is exempt from requiring its own row or detail file. No other paths are allowed. The updater and verifier recognize only this narrow exception, which allows the repository to return to a clean state without an infinite chain of ledger commits.
- Never rewrite a historical subject to remove a name or change its wording. Flag provenance-sensitive terms in the notes instead.
- Link only to records and tracker items for this repository. Do not add sibling-repository paths or dependencies.

Five isolated single-criterion verdicts, all satisfied. Two lessons came out of it. AR-361 binds every citation to one commit, so a retrospective close cannot place evidence in the historical implementation commit; these records bind to the closure commit, cite the current tree for code and tests, and carry the pre-fix reproduction as its own artifact run against 85ad8d88^, where the bridge regression fails assert 2 == 0 and the config parent raises ConfigurationError: configuration parent permits cross-account path substitution with every component left 0775 under umask 0002. And a delegation chain must be cited to its leaf: the verifier refused AR-268 criterion 2 three times, each refusal naming the next helper down, until _secure_storage_parent_component showed the component being restricted and re-asserted trusted. |

| `40f4e3bb` | 2026-09-04 | docs(ar392): file the transport failures that collapse to one code | [AR-392](../roadmap/issue-AR-392-transport-failures-collapse-to-one-code.md) | Item 2 of the AR-383 capsule's next package, filed as AR-392. The deployment residue behind the v2 aliases was split on 2026-09-04 into a runtime timeout below the gateway's, every workforce profile carrying timeout_ms 30000 against deployments carrying timeout 45.0 and observed at 30.04 s on capture391 turn 201 and capture392 turn 205, and one deployment's misplaced brace, a complete 5330-character HTTP 200 body from b0b6f29c failing at character 257 before a candidate's score; both reach the receipt as provider_no_valid_response and nothing separates them. The mechanism is that invoke_structured_provider_result returns a bare None from eleven distinct places and one branch of _invoke_stage stamps a single code on all of them, so a request that was never built, a call the runtime's own deadline aborted, a status-bearing HTTPError that open_no_redirect closes and deliberately re-raises, a body that is not JSON and model text that is not a JSON object are indistinguishable, and the 401, 429 or 502 the gateway returned is discarded by the blanket except. The filing proposes extending the two named answers the runtime already has, the ADR-0199 truncation record and the ADR-0204 credential answer, to the failures between them, with the invariant that failure_reason means no call was made kept explicit: a timeout spends its budget and counts as called, a refusal before the request leaves does not. The timeout ordering stays operator configuration, since the runtime cannot read the deployment's value; what it owes is the elapsed time on the attempt and the effective timeout from doctor. Filed only, no runtime change; the capsule is updated line-neutrally at its 180-line limit and the roadmap mapping row reads pending authorization. |

It is lost in two places, and the second is already solved next door: the hiring stage loop times its own call and, on the same bare None, records provider_call_timed_out when the elapsed time reached the profile's timeout and provider_call_failed otherwise, carrying latency_ms on the failed attempt either way, sound because the deadline handed to the transport is never raised above provider.timeout. _invoke_stage does not time the call, so it cannot make even that outside-in inference and has no latency to record; two stage loops over one transport report the same failure at different resolutions and the coarser one staffs every turn. AR-378 left the deeper request standing in the tree at hiring.py:707, that invoke_structured_provider_result would have to surface why it gave up before a transport error could be told from a schema rejection, which is AR-392 asked for from the other side. The approach now closes the divergence first, costing nothing and separating the two live causes on its own, then does the transport work for the causes the outside-in split cannot see: an HTTP status, a body that is not JSON, model text that is not a JSON object. Acceptance criterion 1 now requires both loops to classify an identical transport failure identically. Filing only, no runtime change. |

Probed through the same retrieve_candidate_union call the eval makes, on ca11182d: install this <url>, Install ripgrep on this machine, configure the gateway, restart the service, troubleshoot why the service will not start and set up monitoring all retrieve nothing at all; upgrade the runtime returns technical-writer alone; while the two card-vocabulary controls put devops-automator first, with devops-automator in the catalog throughout. Two conflations are recorded: retrieval_has_signal and an empty candidate list are different predicates, since the upgrade probe reads as no signal and still returns a candidate; and the p95 runbook prompt, one of the seven the shipped roster reports as zero-signal, has signal against this 16-agent corpus, so a corpus measurement bounds the eval's blindness and not the roster's. The cases are deliberately not added, because as graded cases they would encode a red gate today for the issue itself rather than a regression; they land with the fix or as a reported-but-unthresholded diagnostic set, a change to a versioned evaluation contract and its own decision. ## Provenance notes

- `2434f30` contains the name `Hermes` in its historical subject. The subject is retained exactly as committed for faithful provenance; the name does not create an active cross-repository link or dependency.
- `8f6d320` records a handoff document that was later removed. The subject remains part of the immutable commit record; no deleted document was restored for this worklog.
- `a183594` is a `docs(worklog):` ledger commit that also updated `docs/roadmap/handoffs/issue-AR-235.md`. The worklog-ledger exemption allows only `docs/worklog/**` and the reciprocal roadmap README cell. The capsule refresh should have been a separate `docs(roadmap):` commit. Retained as-is; no history rewrite.
- `56e7dee`, `410c1d1`, `66f62b9`, and `d38e08b` are published `docs(worklog):` commits that also updated `docs/roadmap/issue-AR-119-inference-first-workforce.md`. The mixed commits violate the narrow ledger exemption, but rewriting shared history would be destructive. AR-254 records their exact-SHA grandfathering; future mixed ledger commits still fail.
